The mutation evidence is stronger than usual here: neutering the shared primitive reddens the runtime pin AND the build's own dataset tests, which is what proves the two ends genuinely share one implementation rather than two copies that happen to agree today.

**Owner dispositions recorded, not silently absorbed.** AI/discovery pair publication still performs two sequential writes with no rollback or serialization — all three legs raised it, and it stays OUT of this PR by ruling: it is a PRE-EXISTING publication property, not a contradiction this remediation created, its consequence is already contained (a torn optional pair fails the runtime pair check, the layer goes unavailable, the canonical browser stays usable), and closing it would extend transaction machinery to two more pairs — an artifact-publication design question that deserves one deliberate answer for all three pairs rather than a third hand-rolled rename/backup choreography. Recorded as **known optional-layer publication-hardening debt, non-blocking for #245.** Separately, end-to-end delivery of the stuck-lock warning through the CLI is accepted as a **non-blocking evidence residual** on the owner's stated conditions, each verified: staging never reports a failure as success; a stuck lock does block later publication; the code path does hand the diagnostic to the CLI; and — stated plainly rather than dressed up — **there is no discriminating end-to-end pin for warning delivery.** Returning a single string does not close it (a caller can still split it), and forcing a lock-removal failure inside a subprocess would need a test-only seam in production code, which a standing ruling forbids. It is NOT "E2E verified".

**The verification harness itself failed three times today, always in the direction of reporting success — recorded because that is the most dangerous shape a tooling defect can take.** First a name filter matched zero tests and the run exited 0, so a completely unpinned fix was reported as proven (round 4). Then the validity check parsed only stdout while vitest summarises on stderr, so every run self-reported "0 tests ran". Then, ANSI-stripped and reading both streams, it still mis-parsed the summary whenever EVERY selected test failed, because that form omits the "passed" clause the regex required. Each fix exposed the next. The harness now requires, per mutation: a GREEN baseline that actually ran N tests, and a RED mutant that ran the same N. Re-running the round-6 batch under the working harness re-confirmed seven pins — and falsified one: the canonical publish "snapshot fidelity" mutation had passed only because the earlier mutant failed to compile, and the real mutant did not redden anything, because no test rewrote the source mid-stage. That gap is now closed by a `beforePublish` seam and a pin proven under the fixed harness. **A mutation result is evidence only when the mutant is the defect you mean AND the harness is shown to observe what it claims.** Gates: `vitest run` **1127/1127** (108 files) · typecheck · eslint · prettier · `pnpm schemas`.

**Round 7 on `d3af8d3` (Charter D added: does the runtime enforce every canonical invariant the build does?): DO NOT PROCEED.** Two legs converged on a HIGH that exposes how partial the previous BOM fix was: the artifact half of every pair had been made BOM-consistent, but the META half still went through `Response.json()`, which strips a BOM, while the build kept `Buffer.toString('utf8')` and rejected it — so a BOM-prefixed meta was accepted by all four loaders and refused by all four stagers, and the live smoke in `verify.ts` stripped it too. **Fixing one file of a two-file pair is not fixing the pair.** All four loaders and the smoke now decode meta through one shared BOM-preserving reader. Also fixed: `metaRes.json()` was called before `.catch` attached, recreating for meta exactly the evaluation-order bug already fixed for `fetchImpl` — a synchronous throw escaped untyped.

**The deepest finding was against the evidence method itself, and it changed the design.** A leg defeated the snapshot-fidelity pins with a mutant that re-opened the sources AFTER validation but BEFORE the test seam — and, since a read-back compares against the validated buffer, re-assigning that buffer defeated the read-back too. The conclusion is general: **any injection point the implementation itself invokes can be defeated by acting just before it.** So the property stopped being asserted and became ENFORCED: every one of the four pairs now re-hashes what actually LANDED and compares it against the digest recorded in META — a value no source rewrite and no buffer re-assignment can influence. The canonical meta half, which carries no self-digest, gets a read-back instead, and is labelled as the weaker check it is. Each guard is then pinned by driving it to FIRE, because a detector nobody has watched fire is not evidence.

**Five further evidence defects, each closed:** the destination-`EIO` pin threw for every path and so aborted at the SOURCE probe, pinning the wrong step entirely (now scoped to the dist path); the options allowlist inspected only the first interface declaration, so a MERGED second declaration or an `extends` could hide a bypass (now rejected outright, with a control); the AI/discovery and canonical-meta snapshot properties had no pin; and — the one with real production consequence — **every hook and App test injected a loader, so the branch production actually takes (no loader, fall through to the real loader) had never once executed.** A broken default would have left classification permanently unloaded in production with the entire suite green. Now pinned by driving the hook with no loader against a stubbed transport.

**One boundary stated honestly rather than closed:** with the structural guard in place, a hypothetical re-opening publisher can no longer publish unverified bytes — it fails loudly instead — so which buffer the publisher uses is no longer safety-critical, and the AI/discovery "publishes the validated buffer" detail remains unpinned by design rather than by omission. Gates: `vitest run` **1133/1133** (108 files) · typecheck · eslint · prettier.

**Round 7's third leg added four more, all closed.** The sharpest was that `AGREE-BOM` — the pin for the BOM decode fix — **also passed against the OLD text-hashing implementation**, which rejects the same fixture as an integrity mismatch: the right answer for the wrong reason. It now asserts the failure KIND, so only a byte-digest plus a BOM-preserving decode reaches the parse stage, and it reddens against both broken forms. The build side of the shared `node_id` invariant had no boundary pin, so a call-site mutation keeping only the count problem passed the whole deploy suite. `verifyBuiltArtifact` is a THIRD call site of the canonical byte contract and its tests used ordinary UTF-8 only, so swapping it to decoded-text hashing would have passed — it now carries the same decode-invariant trap as the staging and helper call sites. **And the mutation evidence this record keeps citing is now an auditable artifact rather than a claim:** `scripts/mutation-check.py` runs a named mutation, re-runs a chosen test, and refuses to report a verdict unless the baseline was green AND both runs actually executed tests — encoding, in comments, each of the three ways this harness previously failed by reporting success. Gates: `vitest run` **1136/1136** (108 files) · typecheck · eslint · prettier.

**Charter D, self-audited before Round 8's legs reported — and it found a real defect in Round 7's own new mechanism.** The owner's addition asked not merely whether the post-publish guard DETECTS a mismatch, but what the disk and the caller look like afterwards. Traced and reproduced for the skills pair: the guard sat OUTSIDE the rollback region, after `committed = true`. So on a mismatch it detected correctly and returned `staged: false` — while the corrupt pair stayed LIVE in the dist, the pre-existing pair stayed stranded under `.staging-bak`, and the reason read "any pre-existing pair restored", **a restore that had not happened**. Detect-after-publish is not automatically safe publication, exactly as the charter warned. The guard now runs INSIDE the protected region, before `committed`, so a mismatch takes the ordinary rollback path: the old pair comes back, no residue survives, and the report is true. The pin asserts the CONSEQUENCE, not just the detection, and is mutation-proven both ways — moving the guard back outside the region reddens it, as does removing it. For the canonical pair the containment is different and was verified rather than assumed: the guard THROWS, the CLI exits non-zero, and `pages.yml` runs staging under a default `bash -e`, so the step fails and nothing is uploaded; the runner's dist is built fresh each run, so there is no previous valid pair to restore, and the message never claims one.

**Round 8 on `55618a7` — 3/3 DO NOT PROCEED, with complete convergence on the two headline findings and one genuinely new production defect found while closing them.** All three legs independently reproduced the skills post-publish guard sitting OUTSIDE the rollback region (already caught and fixed by the author's own Charter D audit before the legs reported — an independent double-hit). All three also found that the guards re-hash only the ARTIFACT and never the published META bytes, which **disproves the recorded AI/discovery containment**: changing only `generated_at` after validation leaves the pair internally coherent, so runtime validation has no reason to reject it. Under Charter H that residual is REOPENED and awaits an owner scope ruling. Two further scope questions are likewise open: discovery enforces neither `node_id` uniqueness nor `source_count` agreement (a duplicate `node_id` stages, loads, and reaches a UI that uses it as a React key), and the decoded-text digest class survives at a FIFTH and SIXTH surface — `packages/classifier/src/dataset.ts`, `classifier/src/assemble.ts` and `discovery/src/cli.ts` — which the author's own earlier twin sweep saw and did not follow up.

**The evidence findings closed in this round were, once again, the larger half — and one of them uncovered a real production bug.** Chasing the "production default path is unpinned" finding into an actually-strengthened test revealed WHY it had never been exercised: `crypto.subtle.digest` rejects an `ArrayBuffer` that came from `Response.arrayBuffer()` in another realm — observed verbatim as `2nd argument is not instance of ArrayBuffer` — and the loaders' fail-soft catch turned that into a SILENT `unavailable`. Every existing test injected its own loader, so nothing had ever run that line for real. The digest input is now wrapped in a view, and the default-path pin requires the loader's RESULT to reach `ready` rather than merely observing that a request went out. Also closed: the opt-out surface could be bypassed at the PARAMETER via an intersection type while the interface stayed clean; META BOM parity had no pin at all for any of the four loaders; and a meta stream failure was typed `schema`, rendering a transport reset as "Data failed validation".

**The mutation harness failed a FOURTH time, again by reporting success, and this one invalidated its own prior verdicts.** Its total-parser counted SKIPPED tests as executed, so a filter matching nothing reported "8 skipped (8)" as eight runs; it never required the mutant to run the same number of tests as the baseline; it never checked WHY a mutant died; it had no `finally`, so an interruption left the source mutated; and the committed `CASES` list was empty, so running the file exited 0 without doing anything. All fixed, and the load-bearing mutants are now committed IN the harness so the claims in this record are reproducible from the tree. On its first corrected run it immediately falsified one of its own earlier verdicts: the canonical snapshot mutant died from an uncaught guard error rather than the assertion the test claimed to make. **A harness that has been wrong four times in the same direction earns no trust it has not re-demonstrated.** Gates: `vitest run` **1146/1146** (108 files) · typecheck · eslint · prettier.

**Process defect found and corrected by the author (recorded because it invalidates provenance, not just tidiness):** rounds 2 and 3 ran against the shared worktree while that worktree was being edited, so verdicts nominally frozen to a SHA were partly reading later uncommitted state. One leg detected this and excluded the drift explicitly; that it was caught by a reviewer rather than by the process is the finding. Subsequent rounds run against an isolated checkout of the reviewed SHA.

**Owner adjudication of Round 8's three open scope questions (2026-08-21), executed in the round-9 remediation:**

**R8-A — the reopened AI/discovery containment: MUST FIX, in #245.** The recorded containment ("a torn/tampered optional pair fails the runtime pair check") was DISPROVEN by execution: a post-validation rewrite of ONLY `generated_at` leaves the pair internally coherent, so runtime validation accepts it — the guard proved `published artifact == validated artifact` and never `published meta == validated meta`. Under Charter H the residual reopened; the owner ruled the fix in, small and symmetric: every optional pair's guard now READS BACK the published meta and compares it byte-exactly against THIS invocation's validated snapshot — for the skills pair INSIDE the protected region (before `committed`), so a mismatch takes the ordinary rollback path and the report stays true; for AI/discovery as a `staged:false` whose reason names the residue (publication for those pairs is non-transactional by the standing R6 ruling, so the reason reports what remains rather than implying a removal that never ran). The canonical pair already had the read-back; it now has a driving pin like the rest. **Deliberately NOT required: cross-run meta byte-stability** — `generated_at` still legitimately differs per run; the invariant is per-invocation only (the meta that landed is the meta this invocation validated). Consequence contract, pinned per pair and mutation-proven (guard deleted ⇒ its pin reds; skills guard hoisted past `committed` ⇒ the consequence assertions red): post-publish artifact mismatch → never success; post-publish meta mismatch → never success; failure reasons describe the actual disk state.

**R8-B — discovery `node_id` uniqueness / `source_count` parity: VALID FINDING, OUT-OF-SCOPE for #245 — deferred discovery correctness debt.** A duplicate `node_id` stages, loads, and reaches a UI that uses it as a React key; `source_count: 999` beside one source is displayed as-is. Both are real, and both are PRE-EXISTING discovery semantic-validation gaps — no #245 change made an unreachable invalid state reachable, and #245 makes no whole-artifact-semantic-validation claim for discovery. Owner disposition, recorded verbatim: _B — VALID FINDING / OUT-OF-SCOPE FOR #245. Follow-up discovery contract hardening: `node_id` uniqueness; `source_count` === actual source cardinality; build/runtime parity tests._ Scheduled independently after the #245 merge (before M2.4, or folded into discovery's next maintenance slice). It re-enters #245's gate only if a reviewer proves #245 changed the reachability of an invalid discovery state or added a false normative claim; neither is the case on the current inventory.

**R8-C — the decoded-text digest class at its fifth and sixth surfaces: MUST FIX for acceptance semantics; legitimate semantic fingerprints stay.** The owner's classification rule, applied to every hash site in the repo rather than mechanically byte-ifying string hashes: a hash participating in **accepting/rejecting an existing artifact, checking it against metadata, deciding whether an input may be consumed, or proving copied/deployed bytes → MUST hash exact bytes**; a hash **explicitly defined as a semantic fingerprint of newly canonicalized content, computed after intentional canonical serialization → may remain text-based, with its semantics explicit and never presented as byte integrity**. Fixed to bytes (each with a decode-invariant U+FFFD/0xFF divergence pin whose two-arm proof is a committed harness mutant that IS the old implementation): `classifier/src/dataset.ts` (`loadCanonicalDataset` now takes `starsBytes` — the classifier could previously produce content against a dataset the byte-strict deployed runtime refuses to load), `classifier/src/assemble.ts` (`verifyAiArtifacts` now takes `annotationsBytes`; the meta half stays TEXT deliberately — it carries no self-digest, and its canonical-form-equality check is semantic, failing closed on decode loss), `classifier/src/verify-diff.ts` (`readArtifactAtRef`/`tryReadArtifactAtRef` return exact blob BYTES — the git-fed acceptance gates, provenance and agent gate, hashed a lossy decoding), and `discovery/src/cli.ts` `verify` (an operator's "Valid" no longer contradicts the deploy). The type change forced every call site to migrate (program CLI, prune-orphans, meta-rebase + its CLI, agent-gate `readArtifact` interface — now `Uint8Array`). Classified KEPT, with semantics made explicit where they were not: generation-side digests of just-serialized content written verbatim (`ai-schema` `sha256` + `buildAiAnnotationsMeta`, `skills-generator` meta digests, exporter publish), in-memory canonical-JSON fingerprints that never touch a file (`classifier/fingerprint.ts`, `ai-schema/job.ts`), the M2.1-ratified input-lineage hashes (§4.4 — a documented decision, untouched), and `discovery/pipeline.ts`'s previous-artifact change-detection FALLBACK (now labelled non-integrity in code: it can only suppress a rewrite, and the byte-strict stager/loader refuse the corrupt file downstream). `ai-schema` now exports the `sha256`/`sha256Bytes` pair with the generation-vs-acceptance contract in their doc comments, so the next surface reaches for the right one by name.

**Mutation harness DEMOTED to corroborating evidence (owner ruling, after four consecutive false-success failures).** `scripts/mutation-check.py`'s PASS/KILL output alone no longer carries a load-bearing claim until the harness re-earns trust over a stable history. Every load-bearing mutation claim must additionally show, by inspection of the run: the mutant diff is the targeted defect; baseline and mutant executed the same intended tests (equal counts, >0); the mutant actually exercised the target behaviour; the RED came from the expected assertion, not a compile/setup/other-guard failure; the `finally` restored the source; and `CASES` is non-empty with real executions. The round-9 evidence was produced under this discipline: the three new-guard pins were first observed RED against the pre-fix tree with the predicted failure shape (TDD, not only harness mutants), and all 15 committed cases report RED with `ran == base > 0` and assertion-caused kills.

**Charter additions (owner, for round 9 onward):** **I.** For every published pair: are BOTH the artifact and the meta the exact validated bytes from THIS invocation? **J.** For every hash used in an accept/reject decision: is the hashed representation exactly the representation the downstream consumer/runtime is contractually validating? (Charter H's first residual is superseded accordingly: the optional-pair containment story no longer leans on runtime rejection for the meta half — the build-side read-back is the enforcement.)

**Round-9 remediation record (first freeze).** Sequencing per the owner: R8 reviewed `55618a7` and the head had already moved (`698c6f4`, `767482a`), so even a green R8 would not have constituted frozen-candidate convergence; the adjudication remediation was FROZEN at `5a7c3bb` and a full three-lens isolated round ran against it (clean detached worktree per leg, exact SHA, verified unmodified after each leg). Gates at that tree: `vitest run` **1156/1156** (109 files; +10 tests, +1 file over R8's 1146/108: four GUARD-META pins, CONTROL+BYTES dataset pins, CONTROL+BYTES `verifyAiArtifacts` pins, and the two-test discovery `verify` subprocess pin file) · typecheck · eslint · prettier · `python3 scripts/mutation-check.py` 15/15 valid RED · CI 4/4.

**Round 9 on `5a7c3bb` (luna@max · luna@ultra · sol@max, isolated worktrees) — 3/3 DO NOT PROCEED. Every finding author-reproduced or refuted-with-evidence before fixing; all fixes in the follow-up commit, TDD RED-first.** Convergences: BOTH luna legs independently found the AI/discovery ARTIFACT-half failure reasons silent about residue (the meta half named it, the artifact half did not, while the CLI continues and Pages uploads the corrupt file — runtime byte rejection is the containment, but the deploy report misled); luna@ultra and sol independently found the worst one — **the canonical META was decoded and re-encoded on publish** (`readFileSync(…, 'utf8')` → `Buffer.from(text)`), so a malformed byte inside `dataset_generated_at` (an unconstrained `z.string()`) was silently NORMALIZED to U+FFFD and the read-back compared against the same normalized snapshot: author-reproduced, 190-byte source → 192-byte publication → `staged` success — a direct violation of charter question I on the one pair everyone assumed finished. Fixed: the canonical meta is read as BYTES, published as those bytes, and read back against them (mirroring the optional pairs); validation still parses the decoded text.

**sol@max findings beyond the convergence set:** (1) **canonical form is a BYTE contract too** — `verifyAiArtifacts`'s determinism check compared DECODED text, so a decode-alike byte mutation whose meta was RE-STAMPED with the mutated bytes' correct digest passed the hash gate AND the form gate, accepting artifact bytes that are not the serializer's output; fixed to byte equality, with the consequence that the form gate now fires before the hash gate for such inputs (BYTES pin re-anchored to the actual gate; a new HASH-GATE pin keeps the digest check pinned; the harness mutant for this claim is the form-gate text-compare — a text-hashing mutant at the hash gate is UNREACHABLE once the form gate is byte-strict, and is recorded as such in the CASES comment). (2) **Optional-pair guard interleaving (NEW RECORDED RESIDUAL, owner question):** the two half-guards read the disk at different times, so schedule A-artifact-guard-passes → B overwrites artifact → A meta-guard-passes leaves a MIXED pair on disk reported `staged:true`. Both pairs now verify in ONE read pass, cross-checked as a pair (landed meta == this invocation's snapshot AND landed artifact hashes to that snapshot's digest), which narrows the window to the gap between two adjacent reads — closing it entirely needs the serialization the R6 ruling explicitly reserved to the skills pair. Bounded: a mixed pair is internally INCOHERENT, so the runtime pair check refuses it fail-soft. **Open for the owner: extend pair-level serialization to AI/discovery (reversing the R6 scope bound), or ratify this narrowed, runtime-bounded residual.** (3) **Write-failure residue unnamed** — an EISDIR on the meta path left the freshly-written artifact in the dist with only the errno in the reason; both optional stagers now track whether publication began and append "the dist retains a partial pair" (RESIDUE pins, EISDIR-driven). (4) **Quoted option names evaded the surface extractor** — `'allowUnverified'?: boolean` is legal TypeScript and matched nothing in a bare-identifier regex; the extractor now parses BOTH forms so a quoted bypass fails the allowlist, and the new BEHAVIORAL pin (below) catches any actual read regardless of declaration site. (5) **A SEVENTH decoded-text digest surface: `scripts/smoke-realgit.mjs`** hashed the committed stars.json's decoded text while asserting HASH-2 ("sha must equal committed stars bytes") — the release smoke could certify a byte-corrupt commit that byte-strict deploy/runtime validation rejects; fixed to raw bytes and re-run green. **The author's round-9 twin sweep missed it because the sweep globbed only `*.ts`/`*.py` — recorded as a sweep-method defect, and the re-run sweep now covers `*.mjs`/`*.js`/`*.cjs` (no eighth surface found).**

**luna@ultra's remaining findings:** the OPS-A freshness monitor read the live meta through BOM-stripping `Response.text()`, reporting `fresh` on exact bytes the runtime rejects while the base dashboard fails closed — a false-green production monitor, author-reproduced both sides; it now reads raw bytes decoded the build's way, and a BOM'd live meta alarms as unverifiable (BOM-PARITY pin; the freshness test double became a REAL `Response` because a text-only double cannot express the distinction). And Charter E: the production default `loadStars({ base: import.meta.env.BASE_URL })` wiring in App was exercised by NO test (every App test injects `loader`, every loader test injects `fetchImpl`); a DEFAULT-PATH App pin now serves a real pair over a stubbed global fetch and requires the real loader's result to render, with EXACT-URL assertions (a substring check cannot discriminate a wrong base). **luna@max's evidence finding** — the static no-bypass pins inspect interface text only, so a body-level `(opts as any).skipIntegrity` read stays invisible — is closed BEHAVIORALLY: every loader must complete a fully successful load with its options object proxied to THROW on any property read outside the allowlist, so a bypass consulted under ANY name, declared ANYWHERE, breaks the success path (INTEG-NO-BYPASS-BEHAVIORAL, four loaders; its able-to-fail mutant plants an undeclared read in a loader body).

Gates after the round-9 fixes: `vitest run` **1167/1167** (109 files; +11: BYTES-META, BOM-PARITY, two RESIDUE pins, BYTES-RESTAMPED, HASH-GATE, DEFAULT-PATH, four INTEG-NO-BYPASS-BEHAVIORAL) · typecheck · eslint · prettier · `scripts/mutation-check.py` **19/19** valid RED (each round-9 mutant is the old implementation or the R7 outside-the-region class) · `node scripts/smoke-realgit.mjs` PASSED. Frozen `c7ace7c`, CI 4/4.

**Round 10 on `c7ace7c` (luna@max · luna@ultra · sol@max, isolated worktrees) — 3/3 DO NOT PROCEED, and the legs defeated three of round 9's own remediations. Every finding author-adjudicated; every fix TDD-first or mutation-proven.**

**R10-1 (all three legs, High — the headline): a DETECTED post-publish mismatch still shipped.** The round-9 guards reported `staged:false` and left the pair in the dist; the CLI logs "skipped" and exits 0, `verifyBuiltArtifact` checks only the canonical pair, so Pages UPLOADED the retained pair — and a meta-only rewrite stays internally coherent, so the runtime SERVED it (sol and luna@ultra both reproduced `loadAnnotations`/`loadDiscovery` returning the rewritten timestamp). Detection without a consequence was no containment. FIXED: any failure after publication begins — a detected mismatch on either half, or a thrown write — DISCARDS this invocation's two dist paths (best-effort `rmSync`, outcome reported truthfully: "this run's dist writes were removed" / "could NOT all be removed (residue remains)"). Absence then fails soft at runtime, the correct direction for an optional layer. This is ownership-bounded cleanup of the run's own derived paths — not the forbidden glob/sweep, not a pre-existing-pair restore, not serialization; the skills pair's transactional machinery stays where the R6 ruling put it. The `publishBegan` flag moves BEFORE the first write (luna@ultra: a mid-write ENOSPC/EIO leaves truncation the after-the-write flag missed). Pins rewritten from the retain-contract to the removal-contract (GUARD, GUARD-META, RESIDUE ×2 pairs), with "lying reason" mutants — a reason claiming removal without performing it reddens on the disk assertions.

**R10-2 (luna@max High, luna@ultra Medium): the recorded interleaving residual's boundedness wording was DISPROVEN as stated.** Round 9 recorded "interleaving ⇒ a mixed pair ⇒ internally incoherent ⇒ runtime refuses". Both legs showed the OTHER interleaving outcome: a concurrent stager fully REPLACING the pair with its own validated pair after A's reads — internally coherent, correctly accepted by the runtime, but A's `staged:true` then misattributes whose bytes are live (and, with R10-1's removal, a discard can act on the other invocation's pair). The residual is re-recorded with both outcomes: MIXED ⇒ refused fail-soft; FULL REPLACEMENT ⇒ accepted (it IS a validated pair) with attribution lost. Neither is closable without extending pair-level serialization beyond the skills pair — **the open owner question, now sharper: extend the R6-reserved serialization to the optional pairs (and the canonical pair, which shares the shape), or ratify last-write-wins-with-misattribution among concurrent legitimate stagers, noting the real pipeline is a single stager on a fresh dist per Pages run.**

**R10-3 (sol High + luna@ultra Low, convergent): the round-9 DEFAULT-PATH pins discriminated nothing about the base.** Under vitest `import.meta.env.BASE_URL` IS `/`, so a default that DROPPED `BASE_URL` and hardcoded `/` — the faithful GitHub-Pages regression, where production serves from `/<repo>/` and the canonical fetch would hit the domain root and fail the base dashboard closed — passed every round-9 assertion (sol ran that mutant: 12/12 App tests green). FIXED: both DEFAULT-PATH pins (App and the skills hook) stub a non-root base via `vi.stubEnv('BASE_URL', …)` and assert EXACT request URLs against it, for ALL FOUR layers' defaults (a wrong optional-layer base silently kills that layer in production). The wrong-base mutants for the App wiring and the skills hook wiring are both committed and RED.

**R10-4 (sol High + luna@ultra Medium, convergent): the round-9 behavioral no-bypass pin was defeated two ways, and the extractor a third.** sol's proof-of-concept: declare `readonly allowUnverified?: boolean` (the `readonly` modifier made it INVISIBLE to the extractor's bare-identifier regex) and consult it ONLY on the integrity-failure branch — the round-9 proxy THREW on undeclared reads but ran only the SUCCESS path, so the failure-branch read was never exercised; 56/56 relevant tests stayed green while `{allowUnverified: true}` loaded a stale-digest artifact. luna@ultra added the probe-form gap: `in`/`Object.hasOwn` never hit a `get` trap. FIXED: the extractor now parses quoted AND `readonly`-modified declarations (CONTROL added); the behavioral pin became a RECORDING proxy trapping `get`+`has`+`ownKeys`+`getOwnPropertyDescriptor`, run through BOTH the fully-successful load AND the integrity-failure load per loader, with recorded out-of-allowlist probes asserted EMPTY after each — a bypass consulted under any name, via any probe form, on either path, lands in the record whatever the outcome. sol's exact failure-branch mutant is committed and RED (`ran=4`, killed by the record assertion).

**R10-5 (sol Medium): AI/discovery source probes were the round-6 fail-open shape, unswept.** Both used `existsSync`, which reports false for EACCES/EIO — the class fixed for the skills pair in round 6 had been left on both siblings, and AI additionally read a half-pair as "no AI artifacts present". FIXED: shared ENOENT-only `lstat` probes (injectable via `OptionalPairHooks.lstatImpl`, the established seam precedent), a probe error surfaces as "could not probe its sources — …", and AI gains the absent-vs-incomplete distinction discovery already had. PROBE-ENOENT-ONLY pins for both pairs; fail-open reintroduction mutants for both are RED.

Gates after the round-10 fixes: `vitest run` **1175/1175** (109 files; +8: INCOMPLETE, PROBE-ENOENT-ONLY ×2, the four behavioral pins doubled to success+failure runs, and the pin rewrites) · typecheck · eslint · prettier · `scripts/mutation-check.py` **25/25** valid RED. Frozen `ea8a3c9`, CI 4/4.

**Round 11 on `ea8a3c9` (luna@max · luna@ultra · sol@max, isolated worktrees; the first sol run died to a PROVIDER content filter while constructing its bypass probe — 397k tokens, empty output, recorded as a missing lens and retried fresh, completing normally) — 3/3 DO NOT PROCEED, converging on ONE production finding plus evidence depth.**

**R11-1 (all three legs, High): a truthful residue REPORT was still not a CONSEQUENCE.** Round 10's discard-on-failure told the truth when `rmSync` itself failed ("could NOT all be removed — residue remains") — and then the CLI exited 0, canonical-only verification passed, Pages uploaded the residue, and the runtime served the coherent rewritten pair. sol reproduced it for ALL THREE optional pairs (read-only dist; each stager returned `staged:false` with the residue wording; all three loaders then served the rewritten timestamps). FIXED as a structured escalation: every stager result now carries `residue?: boolean` — set exactly when this run leaves content it tried and FAILED to expunge (AI/discovery: a stuck discard; skills: `restoreFailed`/`publishedResidue`; the lock is NOT residue, it is never served) — and the CLI `stage` command exits **4** when any result carries it, which `pages.yml`'s `bash -e` turns into "nothing ships". The fail-soft boundary is now principled: **absence and invalid sources stay fail-soft; unremovable REJECTED bytes are a dist-integrity failure and fail the deploy.** Pinned at three levels, each RED-first: the structured flag on the EISDIR paths of both optional pairs and on both skills residue branches, a clean discard asserted NOT to set it, and an end-to-end CLI subprocess pin (fixture data + a directory squatting on the dist meta path → exit 4 + "refusing to let this dist ship"). Mutants: a stager dropping the flag while keeping the truthful prose, and the CLI printing everything and exiting 0 — the round-11 defect itself.

**R11-2 (luna@max + sol, convergent, High): the no-bypass evidence was defeated a third time — closed at the declaration, the probe, and the prototype.** sol's PoC: a `unique symbol` computed option (`[allowUnverified]?: boolean`) is invisible to any string-based extractor AND (on the reviewed head) to a string-only recording proxy — it drove a working text()-instead-of-bytes bypass with the full surface/byte suite green at identical counts. luna@max added the probe forms: `Object.getPrototypeOf(opts)?.skipIntegrity` and symbol reads hit no `get`-string trap. CLOSED: the recording proxy now traps `getPrototypeOf` (recorded as a probe) and records every non-well-known SYMBOL probe; the extractor REJECTS index signatures and computed non-literal keys outright (an unenumerable or symbol-keyed surface cannot be allowlisted — same hard-fail class as `extends`/merged declarations) while extracting quoted, `readonly`-modified, and computed string-literal forms; CONTROLs cover every form. Committed mutants: the prototype-walk probe and the symbol-keyed probe, both killed by the record assertions.

Gates after the round-11 fixes: `vitest run` **1178/1178** (109 files; +3: RESIDUE-EXIT CLI pin, the index-signature/computed-key CONTROLs) with the residue assertions folded into five existing pins · typecheck · eslint · prettier · `scripts/mutation-check.py` **29/29** valid RED. Frozen `c3437db`, CI 4/4.

**Round 12 on `c3437db` (luna@max · luna@ultra · sol@max, isolated worktrees) — 3/3 DO NOT PROCEED. All three legs converged on the residue machinery's remaining edge; the production findings are now sub-cases of the R11 contract rather than new classes, plus one PRE-EXISTING divergence deferred.**

**R12-1 (all three legs, the one production finding): the skills stager's OWN cleanup failures were swallowed — the R11 residue contract had a hole on the transactional pair.** R11 wired `residue` on the optional pairs and on the skills abort's `restoreFailed`/`publishedResidue`, but a failed discard of a skills `.staging-tmp` (abort path) or `.staging-bak` (SUCCESS path) set nothing: a successful publication returned `staged:true` with a stale backup left in the public dist, and the CLI exited 0. FIXED: a `cleanupResidue` flag feeds `residue` on both paths — the success-path backup discard and the abort-path temp discard now report failure. Pinned at the unit level (a directory can't be pre-placed at a token-randomized path from outside, so the pins use the `afterCommit`/`afterStageWrite` seams to replace this run's own `.staging-bak`/`.staging-tmp` with a non-empty directory the non-recursive force-rm can't remove); both mutants RED.

**R12-2 (sol, evidence): the residue escalation was pinned only for AI end-to-end.** A mutant dropping `discovery.residue` from the CLI's `.some()` condition passed both CLI tests. FIXED: the RESIDUE-EXIT subprocess pin is parametrized over AI AND discovery (a directory squatting each layer's dist meta path → exit 4). Skills is deliberately not end-to-end here — its transactional stager cleans up robustly, so an unremovable-residue state is not reachable by pre-placing a directory from a black-box subprocess; its residue branches are pinned directly instead (recorded, not hidden). A "drop discovery from the escalation array" mutant is RED against the discovery case.

**R12-3 (sol + luna@ultra + luna@max, evidence): the no-bypass proxy exempted well-known symbols, and the extractor missed computed keys.** sol drove a working bypass via `opts[Symbol.iterator]` (a well-known symbol the R11 proxy exempted) and via a `unique symbol` computed key (`[allowUnverified]?:`, invisible to the string extractor); luna@max added `Object.getPrototypeOf(opts)?.x` and non-well-known symbols. A clean load was verified to touch NO symbol on `opts`, so the exemption was unnecessary as well as unsafe. FIXED: the recording proxy records EVERY symbol probe and traps `getPrototypeOf`; the extractor rejects index signatures and computed non-literal keys outright and extracts quoted/`readonly`/computed-string-literal names. Mutants for a well-known-symbol probe, a symbol-keyed probe, and a prototype-walk probe are all RED.

**R12-4 (sol + luna@max + luna@ultra, evidence): the production DEFAULT-PATH pin never exercised the optional ARTIFACT fetches.** Serving 404 for the optional layers meant each loader fetched only its META, so a default that dropped `BASE_URL` on the artifact fetch (`${base}…` → `/…`) passed. FIXED: the App DEFAULT-PATH pin serves schema-valid optional METAs so each loader PROCEEDS to request its artifact (the body may 404 and fail soft, but the request is recorded), and asserts the EXACT base-carrying artifact URL for all three optional layers. A wrong-base-on-the-AI-artifact mutant is RED.

**R12-5 (luna@ultra, NEW — VALID FINDING, OUT-OF-SCOPE for #245, DEFERRED, awaiting owner ratification): AI acceptance diverges on CANONICAL FORM (not bytes).** `verifyAiArtifacts` (the build/provenance verifier) requires the artifact bytes to BE the canonical serialization (`=== serializeAnnotations(...)`), while `stageAiArtifacts` and `loadAnnotations` require only a matching BYTE digest + schema — so a compact-but-digest-consistent AI pair is REJECTED by the build verifier yet STAGED and SERVED (author-reproduced: `not deterministically serialized` from the verifier, digest matches for staging/runtime). This is the R8-B disposition class, and bounded further: (a) it is a canonical-FORM divergence, not the byte-integrity divergence #245 remediates — byte integrity now agrees across build/deploy/runtime; (b) #245 did not create it — the canonical-form check has ALWAYS lived only in `verifyAiArtifacts`, never in staging/runtime; (c) it is contained by the provenance gate (`verifyAiProvenanceFromGit` runs `verifyAiArtifacts` as a required check on the PR→main trust boundary), so a non-canonical AI pair cannot reach the deployed dist through the normal path; (d) enforcing canonical serialization at runtime would drag the serializer into the crypto/serializer-free browser `contracts` bundle — a deliberate design the loader avoids. Disposition: recorded as deferred AI-acceptance-contract debt alongside the R8-B discovery gaps; it re-enters #245 only if a reviewer shows #245 made a previously unreachable non-canonical state reachable, or that the provenance gate does not in fact bound it. NOT fixed here.

Gates after the round-12 fixes: `vitest run` **1181/1181** (109 files; +3: two skills CLEANUP-RESIDUE pins, the discovery RESIDUE-EXIT case, the computed-key CONTROL, with the App/proxy pins hardened in place) · typecheck · eslint · prettier · `scripts/mutation-check.py` **34/34** valid RED. Frozen `9ce50fc`, CI 4/4.

**Round 13 on `9ce50fc` (luna@max · luna@ultra · sol@max, isolated worktrees) — 3/3 DO NOT PROCEED. All three converged on ONE production defect (cleanup precision under a single I/O failure); the rest were evidence gaps. This is the I/O-failure-cleanup long tail, not a new architectural class.**

**R13-1 (all three legs): a mid-write partial temp was left unreported.** The skills stager recorded each `.staging-tmp` in `created` only AFTER `writeFileSync` returned, so a write that failed PARTWAY (ENOSPC/EFBIG — all three legs reproduced with `ulimit -f`) left OUR partial temp on disk unregistered: the abort path never discarded it, `residue` stayed unset, and the CLI (exit 0) uploaded the leftover. This disproved the R5 "double-I/O-failure only" containment — a single failed write suffices. FIXED: a `writeTempExclusive` helper registers the path for cleanup on ANY non-`EEXIST` throw (the exclusive open having succeeded proves the partial is ours; `EEXIST` created nothing and is never ours), so a mid-write throw's partial is discarded and, if the discard also fails, reported. Pinned via a `writeTempImpl` seam (the `lstatImpl`/`openImpl` precedent for fs errors a real filesystem cannot produce on demand); mutant = the old push-after-write.

**R13-2 (sol, High — destructive): the optional discard deleted a pre-existing pair it never wrote.** `publishBegan` discarded BOTH dist paths on any post-flag failure, so a first write that failed at OPEN (EACCES on a read-only pre-existing artifact) DELETED the untouched valid pair and still reported success (sol reproduced through the real CLI). Production-bounded (Pages builds a fresh dist per run, so no pre-existing pair), but destructive on a reused/local dist and falsely reported. FIXED: `publishBegan` is gone; both optional stagers SNAPSHOT their destinations before writing and remove ONLY paths whose bytes CHANGED from the snapshot (errno-free, correct for every case: unchanged ⇒ not ours ⇒ left; changed/new ⇒ ours ⇒ removed). ZERO-WRITE pins (read-only pre-existing artifact → first write EACCES → both files survive, no residue) and STUCK-RESIDUE pins (our own write un-removable via a read-only dist dir → residue) for both pairs; mutant = discard a fixed pair ignoring the snapshot.

**Consequence of the ownership fix, recorded honestly:** a stager now reports `residue` only for its OWN un-removable write, which requires a read-only dist directory that would already have failed the canonical write — so an end-to-end residue can no longer be constructed from a black-box subprocess (the same wall as the skills pair). The R12 subprocess RESIDUE-EXIT pin is therefore replaced by an exported `distHasUnshippableResidue` predicate unit-pinned over every layer position (mutant = check only the first), with the CLI call-site's completeness inspection-covered (K7 proves the command calls each stager) — the analogue of the skills-residue disposition. The stagers' residue-SETTING stays directly pinned (optional STUCK-RESIDUE; skills CLEANUP-RESIDUE / PARTIAL-TEMP). The RESIDUE test's old premise (a foreign directory at the meta path yields residue) is corrected: we clean our artifact and LEAVE the foreign directory, so residue is false there — the ownership fix made it so.

**R13-3 (sol + luna@max, evidence): the no-bypass surface had two more escape shapes.** A SECOND positional parameter (`loadStars(opts = {}, allowUnverified = false)`, sol) was invisible because the extractor captured only the first type; a METHOD-form option (`allowUnverified?(): boolean`, luna@max) was invisible because the extractor's terminator required `:`. FIXED: the entry-point extractor now asserts single arity (rejects a second parameter), and the declaration extractor accepts `:` OR `(` so a method-form option is seen and fails the allowlist; CONTROLs and mutants for both.

**R13-4 (all three, evidence): two R12 harness mutants died for the wrong reason.** They referenced a `discard(...)` helper removed for lint, so they died by `ReferenceError` (the harness misclassified it as an assertion kill). FIXED to the faithful `void discardOk(...)` swallow. This is exactly the six-point-inspection discipline the harness demotion exists to enforce — caught and corrected.

Gates after the round-13 fixes: `vitest run` **1191/1191** (110 files; +10: PARTIAL-TEMP, ZERO-WRITE ×2, STUCK-RESIDUE ×2, revised RESIDUE ×2, the residue-decision unit suite, and the method-form / second-param CONTROLs) · typecheck · eslint · prettier · `scripts/mutation-check.py` **35/35** valid RED. Frozen `5365ccc`, CI 4/4.

**Owner rulings (2026-08-21), recorded on-branch as a doc-only commit BEFORE Round 14 so the reviewed head equals the merge candidate (the `22c29b9` pattern); the agent had stopped the loop and handed these up per operational-rigor §6 (productive-but-unconverging).**

**RULING 1 — Optional AI/discovery concurrency: ACCEPT the bounded last-write-wins residual; do NOT expand transaction machinery into #245.** The two optional pairs stay non-transactional (no lock/backup/rollback). The known concurrency outcomes are exactly two, and both are contained: an interleave into an INCOHERENT pair is rejected by runtime integrity/pair validation and the optional layer fails soft to unavailable; a concurrent stager fully OVERWRITING the pair with its own COHERENT validated pair yields valid data, with only an attribution mismatch for the earlier invocation about whose bytes are finally on disk. The normative contract, verbatim:

> Optional-pair staging is not linearizable under concurrent invocations.
>
> Concurrent publication is outside the guaranteed single-invocation contract:
>
> - a coherent later writer may win;
> - an incoherent interleave must be rejected by runtime validation;
> - no invalid/torn pair may silently become ready.
>
> `staged:true` means this invocation completed its validated publication path; it does not guarantee that no concurrent valid writer replaced it afterward.

Rationale (owner): 13 rounds proved that each added filesystem-choreography step opens a new cleanup/ownership/failure window; replicating the skills transaction state machine to close a non-production-critical, already-fail-soft-contained attribution residual is a bad risk trade. Concurrent-stager linearizability, if ever wanted, is a separate publication-architecture change — never two more hand-rolled transactions grown inside #245. The skills lock/backup/rollback machinery is NOT extended to the optional pairs.

**RULING 2 — R12-5 AI canonical-form divergence: VALID and DEFERRED; do NOT claim build/runtime canonical-form parity.** The finding is real and was reproduced: the AI build verifier requires the bytes to BE the canonical serialization, while AI staging/runtime accept any byte representation whose digest + meta are consistent, so a compact-but-consistent pair is accepted by runtime and refused by the build verifier. Deferred because #245's subject is BYTE INTEGRITY (now agreeing on both ends), the canonical-form check is a pre-existing build-only invariant #245 did not create, the normal PR→main path still has the build/provenance gate blocking a non-canonical artifact from shipping, and moving the canonical serializer into the browser runtime would expand the bundle/runtime contract out of proportion to M2.3. Disposition, verbatim:

> R12-5 — VALID / DEFERRED
>
> Do not claim build/runtime canonical-form parity.
>
> Current guaranteed parity: exact-byte integrity + pair consistency
>
> Deferred: canonical serialization-form parity for AI artifacts

Independent AI-artifact-contract debt, scheduled like the R8-B discovery gaps; does NOT block #245.

**RULING 3 — `#245 @ 5365ccc`: DO NOT MERGE YET; run one full Round 14 on this exact frozen SHA, then converge.** `5365ccc` fixed R13's last production defect but has not itself had a full independent PROCEED round (R13 reviewed `9ce50fc`, 3/3 DO NOT PROCEED). The standing closure condition holds: the final merge candidate must have at least one full independent round that actually PROCEEDs. **Round 14 adds NO new charter** — the mature charter is frozen (remediation-created filesystem windows; ownership/cleanup precision; byte/decode/build-runtime parity; publication consequence; production default path; no-bypass surface; mutation evidence RED-for-the-claimed-reason; recorded-residual containment still holding), and Rulings 1–2 above are recorded so reviewers do not re-raise the accepted residual or the deferred divergence.

**Round-14 triage rule (owner, locks the loop against perpetual motion):** a reviewer's non-correctness output does NOT auto-mutate the tree — wording polish, non-load-bearing test nice-to-haves, mutation-harness generality improvements, and purely-theoretical hardening of an already-recorded residual are acknowledged, not acted on. Only two evidence-finding classes are blockers: (1) it proves a load-bearing correctness claim was never actually verified; (2) it shows a real production regression can recur while the existing gates stay all-green. **Stopping rule:** `3/3 PROCEED with no surviving correctness finding → closure achieved → do NOT voluntarily open Round 15.\*\*

**Round 14 on `33aa267` (closure review) = 3/3 DO NOT PROCEED, converged on ONE root defect; owner ruled A (temp+rename), executed.** All three legs found the optional AI/discovery stagers OVERWROTE dist destinations in place (`writeFileSync(dest, …)`), so on a pre-existing pair + a single I/O failure — NON-concurrent, hence in-scope per Ruling 1's own single-invocation-data-loss exception — data was lost: (luna@max + luna@ultra, 2/3) a read-only meta destination let the artifact overwrite succeed and the meta write EACCES, leaving a torn pair (old orphaned meta) uploaded, `staged:false`, no residue; (sol, worst) a symlinked destination made the write FOLLOW the link and corrupt its EXTERNAL target while cleanup removed only the link — the ownership claim "remove only what THIS run changed" was false for symlinks. Both author-reproduced. Bounded to reused/local dist (production Pages builds a fresh dist per run), but a real correctness defect all three flagged. **Owner ruling A (narrow): fix the single-invocation data-loss window with ownership-safe temp+rename; do NOT reopen the transactional-publication framework.** Executed: a shared `publishOptionalPairAtomically` writes THIS invocation's validated bytes to unique-token `.staging-tmp` files, verifies them, and PUBLISHES BY RENAME — a rename swaps the directory entry, so it never follows a destination symlink (link replaced, target untouched — verified by execution), never truncates a read-only destination in place (the read-only-meta case now SUCCEEDS with a complete new pair), and never destroys a pre-existing pair before the swap (a write/copy failure leaves the old files byte-identical). Cleanup removes ONLY the temporaries this invocation exclusively created (a stuck temp → `residue`); the post-publish exact-byte read-back is preserved (a landed mismatch = a concurrent writer, the accepted last-write-wins residual, reported `staged:false`, the destination left as it is not a temp we own). Authorization honored to the letter: NO pair-level lock, NO move-aside/backup ledger, NO rollback machinery, NO glob cleanup, NO serialization — the concurrency ruling stands unchanged. Applied to BOTH halves of BOTH pairs; the old snapshot/discard-only-changed logic (its own R13 source of these bugs) is removed. Pinned per pair: the owner's three discriminating cases (write-failure never partially overwrites; a success lands exactly the validated bytes; cleanup removes only this run's temp, a foreign temp-suffixed file survives) plus SYMLINK / READONLY-DEST / PARTIAL-TEMP / STUCK-TEMP-RESIDUE / GUARD / GUARD-META; five temp+rename mutants all valid RED. Gates: `vitest run` **1199/1199** (110 files) · typecheck · eslint · prettier · `scripts/mutation-check.py` **35/35** valid RED · `node scripts/smoke-realgit.mjs` PASSED. New SHA frozen; a full isolated Round 15 runs on it — 3/3 PROCEED with no surviving correctness finding → closure, no voluntary R16, back to the merge gate.

**Round 15 on `5d9333f` = 3/3 DO NOT PROCEED, converged on ONE finding: the two-rename torn window; fixed (still within ruling A — no new machinery).** All three legs found that `publishOptionalPairAtomically` renamed the artifact then the meta as two independent steps, so if the SECOND rename failed after the FIRST swapped (luna@max + luna@ultra: a directory obstructing the meta destination → EISDIR; sol: an injected EPERM on the meta rename), the artifact was published, the meta was not, and the catch cleaned only temps — leaving a torn pair with the old artifact destroyed, `staged:false`, no `residue`, CLI exit 0. Single-invocation, in-scope per Ruling 1. This also falsified my round-14 note that a torn pair could "only follow a crash" — a rename FAILURE reaches it too (author-reproduced). Fixed with two guards, no lock/backup/rollback added: (1) a DIRECTORY pre-check — before any rename, `lstatSync` each destination and REFUSE if it is a directory (a symlink is fine, rename replaces the link entry), so the common obstruction is caught with NOTHING published and the pre-existing pair untouched (luna's case → clean fail-soft, verified by execution); (2) a torn-pair SAFETY NET — the second rename is wrapped so a failure after the first sets `residue` (the CLI then exits 4 and `bash -e` ships nothing, rather than uploading a torn half-pair), covering sol's injected case and the genuine crash-between-renames residual. Pinned per pair: TORN-PRECHECK (a directory destination → refused, artifact not published, no residue) and TORN-SAFETY-NET (an injected second-rename failure → residue, via a new `renameImpl` seam — the lstatImpl/openImpl/writeTempImpl precedent); mutants for both (pre-check dropped ⇒ TORN-PRECHECK reds; safety net dropped ⇒ TORN-SAFETY-NET reds) valid RED. The residual now is precisely the crash-between-two-renames the safety net FLAGS as residue (POSIX has no atomic two-file rename), plus the accepted last-write-wins concurrency residual — both bounded, neither uploading a silent torn pair. Gates: `vitest run` **1203/1203** (110 files) · typecheck · eslint · prettier · `scripts/mutation-check.py` **37/37** valid RED · `node scripts/smoke-realgit.mjs` PASSED. Frozen `a332392`, CI 4/4.

**Round 16 on `a332392` (closure review) = 3/3 PROCEED — CLOSURE ACHIEVED.** The first all-PROCEED round in the sequence, on the frozen final candidate (code `5d9333f` + the R15 torn-window fix). All three legs — sol@max, luna@max, luna@ultra — independently verified the exact SHA byte-identical, audited the full frozen charter (remediation-created filesystem windows; four-pair byte/decode/meta acceptance parity; post-publish guards both halves; production default paths; ownership/cleanup precision incl. the R15 pre-check and torn-pair safety net; residue escalation; the integrity opt-out surface incl. symbol/prototype/computed-key/second-parameter/method-form forms), ran the full gate suite green (vitest 1203/1203 bar the one sandbox `listen EPERM`, typecheck, eslint, prettier) and the mutation harness (37/37 valid RED, both R15 mutants included), and found NO surviving correctness finding — no blocker of either class the triage rule admits. luna@ultra explicitly confirmed it did NOT reopen the accepted last-write-wins concurrency residual, the deferred canonical-form divergence (Ruling 2), or the recorded crash-between-renames residual. Per the owner's stopping rule (`3/3 PROCEED with no surviving correctness finding → closure achieved → do NOT voluntarily open a further round → back to the merge gate`), **`a332392` is the merge-ready candidate.** Merge is the OWNER's action (the hard gate: the agent ran the review, so it does not merge). This record is a doc-only, post-verdict status note; the reviewed diff is this commit minus this note (the code is byte-identical to `a332392`).

**Sixteen-round trajectory (closed):** new production-defect classes 6 (R9) → 5 (R10) → 1 (R11) → 1 (R12) → 2 (R13) → 1 (R14) → 1 (R15) → **0 (R16)**, converging from the byte-integrity/decode/build-runtime-parity core through the residue-consequence contract, the no-bypass surface, the optional-pair cleanup-ownership precision (snapshot → temp+rename), and finally the two-rename atomicity window — each fix TDD RED-first, mutation-proven, and isolated-worktree reviewed at the frozen SHA. Standing residuals, all owner-adjudicated and bounded: the last-write-wins concurrency residual (Ruling 1, runtime-rejects an incoherent interleave); the AI canonical-form divergence (Ruling 2, deferred debt, provenance-gate-bounded); the crash-between-two-renames torn pair (flagged as residue → deploy fails, never a silent upload). Deferred follow-ups, out of #245: discovery `node_id` uniqueness / `source_count` parity (R8-B); the canonical-form parity (R12-5).

### 4.11 M2.4 UI projection — first sub-slice: scope + facet + badges + requested/effective semantics (owner-scoped 2026-09-02)

**Sub-slice boundary (owner directive 2026-09-02, "只做" enumeration):** of the §3 M2.4 charter, THIS sub-slice ships exactly the **Skills-ecosystem scope**, the **Skill-category facet**, **card badges**, and the **requested/effective semantics** across `loading | ready | unavailable` — plus the §2.1 soft provenance note, which is part of surfacing the layer and needs no loader change (`stars_sha256` is already on the loaded dataset meta; §4.10 locked decision 3 exposed the classification side for exactly this). **Sequenced next within M2.4, deliberately NOT here:** search enrichment (§7's second code path — `buildSearchText` deliberately does not read `skills` in this sub-slice; a reviewer finding classification labels unsearchable is seeing the sequencing, not an omission), the per-repo classification `summary` display and its search-corpus entry (the record's `summary` is JOINED onto `DerivedRepo.skills` in this sub-slice but no shipped surface renders it yet — carried payload for the next sub-slice, not dead data and not a missing card line), the same-origin `.md` download, and the 3-number coverage line (§5 — the loader already exposes `coverage`, nothing consumes it yet). Group-by-primary stays "later" (§3). The two deferred debts stay independently scheduled and untouched (owner 2026-09-02): discovery `node_id`/`source_count` hardening (R8-B — its recorded "before M2.4" option was not exercised; re-entry criteria unchanged) and AI canonical-form parity (R12-5). M2.3's loader/staging surfaces are not reopened (no current-main evidence of a contract regression; orientation 2026-09-02: the 31 post-merge commits touched only the four data artifacts).

**Canonical state contract (§6 amendment — `scope`/`skill` arrive, as reserved):**

- `scope` ∈ `all | skills`, default `all` (omitted from the URL); invalid → default, like every scalar enum. `skills` = only repos carrying a classification record. **The URL token is UI-canonical (`skills`), deliberately NOT the artifact `scope.id`:** the codec is static and data-free at decode time (§6), and the artifact carries exactly one scope; a second scope value would be a vocabulary-expansion event (the D2 rollout pattern), not data drift.
- `skillCategories: string[]`, default `[]` — the Skill-category multi-select; URL param `skill` (singular, repeated — the array-facet convention), values are category id slugs (§4.2). Unknown/stale ids are **preserved** like every other data-dependent array facet (a valid bookmark that matches nothing until the taxonomy changes), deduplicated + sorted; they are NOT validated against the loaded taxonomy at decode time (the codec stays data-free).
- **Emit order:** `view`, `scope`, `skill`, then the existing P1 order, then `density`, `page` — the §6 conceptual prefix, now locked.
- **Page-reset (§6.3):** `scope` and `skillCategories` join the semantic reset set (genuine value change, not key presence; an explicit `page` in the same update still wins). **Clear-all** resets both (they are query-axis narrowing, not display preference — unlike `view`/`density`, which clear-all preserves).

**Filter semantics when `ready` (§2.2 / §7):** AND across facets, OR within. A repo matches the skill facet iff any selected id ∈ {`primaryCategoryId`} ∪ `secondaryCategoryIds`. Scope and facet each exclude repos without a classification record — **correct narrowing, not suppression** (the M0-FS-3 analog: `ready`-but-unclassified is an ordinary non-match; "absence is not a classification" governs display, not filtering).

**Requested/effective semantics when NOT `ready` (the §2.2 contract, wired here — §4.5's closing paragraph discharged):**

| Condition (layer not `ready`)                  | Behavior                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ---------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `scope=skills` and/or `skill=…` in the URL     | requested values RETAINED in URL and state; neither is applied (base repos never suppressed; **results are never zeroed by the optional layer's absence**)               |
| result summary / Filters count                 | suppressed skills values do NOT count as effective filters (no "· filtered" from them; the toolbar count excludes them — the M0/R3 pattern)                              |
| degraded surface                               | explicit notice, `loading`-specific wording vs `unavailable` wording (M0-FS-6 analog); chips for the requested values remain visible and removable (recoverability)      |
| sidebar facet section                          | hidden while not `ready` (the M0 AI-facet precedent — removal stays possible via chips; the URL is the recovery path)                                                    |
| badges                                         | none (the base card renders byte-identically to a layer-absent render)                                                                                                   |
| search / sort / pagination / density / base UI | fully functional, unchanged — the acceptance gate: classification unavailability must leave the base Starred browser indistinguishable from "no skills request" behavior |

**Facet UI (`ready`):** the Skill-category options are the FULL taxonomy in canonical `order` from the loaded artifact (never data-mined from repos — the taxonomy is the contract, §4.2 I-5; a category with zero live matches is a legitimate empty facet). Section title **"Skills ecosystem"**, facet legend **"Skill category"** (§1 locked naming). The scope control is a single checkbox inside that section. Chips label skill values by taxonomy label when `ready` and by id slug otherwise (the id is canonical and readable; label-resolution for degraded chips is polish, not contract).

**Badges (§8, v1):** a classified repo's card shows its primary-category badge then its ≤1 secondary badge (artifact order), full labels from the taxonomy by id (id slug as fallback if a record references a label the map lacks — unreachable under I-4, defensive display only). No badge when the layer is not `ready` or the repo is unclassified. §8's compact `[+N]` form becomes relevant only if a future taxonomy raises the v1 secondary cap — not built now (no consumer).

**Provenance soft note (§2.1, discharged here):** when the layer is `ready` and `generatedAgainstStarsSha256 ≠` the live dataset's `stars_sha256`, the Skills-ecosystem section carries a muted "generated against an older snapshot of the starred dataset" line. Never a gate, never a warning banner: under daily star sync this mismatch is the steady state, so it lives where the layer is surfaced, not as a global banner.

**M2.3 non-propagation pin superseded (recorded contract edit):** §4.10's "production UI delta = 0" App pin (SKILLS-2 DOM byte-equality across layer states) certified M2.3's "nothing consumes it yet" — M2.4's whole point is to end that state. The pin is REPLACED by the M2.4 acceptance rows below (ready ≠ unavailable DOM is now the _contract_, while unavailable-vs-absent DOM equality is retained). SKILLS-1 (wiring) and SKILLS-3 (matrix row 10: unclassified repo renders as a normal repo) remain valid and are kept.

**Acceptance criteria (each pinned by a named test):**

| ID        | Test                                                                                                                                                                   |
| --------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| M24-FS-1  | `dashboardToView(state, aiReady, skillsReady=false)` neutralizes `scope`+`skillCategories`, preserves every other filter (M0-FS-1 analog)                              |
| M24-FS-2  | `?scope=skills&skill=…` + layer `unavailable` → ALL base repos render (never 0, never narrowed), no "· filtered" from skills, unavailable-wording notice, URL retained |
| M24-FS-3  | same + layer `loading` → loading-specific wording (never "unavailable"), same preservation                                                                             |
| M24-FS-4  | layer `ready`: scope narrows to classified-only; skill facet matches primary OR secondary; `ready`-but-unclassified repo is correctly excluded (M0-FS-3 analog)        |
| M24-FS-5  | layer `unavailable` + requested skills state: search, sort, pagination and density all operate normally on the un-narrowed base result set                             |
| M24-CNT-1 | suppressed skills values excluded from the toolbar Filters count and the "· filtered" summary; counted when `ready`; chips visible + removable in both                 |
| M24-URL-1 | codec round-trip for `scope`/`skill`; emit order `view, scope, skill, …`; defaults omitted; invalid `scope` → `all`; unknown `skill` ids preserved, deduped + sorted   |
| M24-RST-1 | `scope`/`skillCategories` value change resets `page → 1`; a no-op re-set does not; explicit `page` wins; clear-all clears both while preserving `view`/`density`       |
| M24-BDG-1 | `ready` + classified → primary (+ secondary) badge labels; not-`ready` → no skill badges; `ready` + unclassified → no badges                                           |
| M24-PRV-1 | `ready` + hash mismatch → soft note rendered; hash match → absent; not-`ready` → absent                                                                                |

**M2.4 first-sub-slice record (2026-09-02, `feat/m2.4-classification-ui`, cut from `origin/main@8971ec3`)** — deliverable: canonical `scope`/`skillCategories` state + `scope`/`skill` URL codec (§6 amendments: emit order, §6.3 reset set, clear-all clears both); `DerivedRepo.skills` node_id join (the `ai` mirror in `deriveRepo`/`prepareRepositories`); `FilterState.skillsScope`/`skillCategories` with primary-OR-secondary matching; `dashboardToView(state, aiReady, skillsReady)` neutralization; RepositoryView projection — readiness gate on the join map (a not-ready layer structurally cannot badge or filter), suppressed-count exclusion, per-status degraded notices, taxonomy label map, and the §2.1 soft note computed from the live `stars_sha256` already on the loaded dataset meta (NO loader surface reopened); "Skills ecosystem" sidebar/drawer section (scope checkbox + full-taxonomy "Skill category" facet in canonical order, hidden while not ready per the M0 precedent); chips for scope + skill (taxonomy labels when ready, id slugs degraded, removable in both states); card badges (primary + ≤1 secondary, taxonomy labels, none when not ready or unclassified). Search enrichment deliberately NOT wired (SEQ-1 pins the absence — §7 stays a distinct code path for the next sub-slice). The M2.3 SKILLS-2 delta-0 App pin is superseded as recorded above; SKILLS-1 is kept verbatim and SKILLS-3 re-anchored to card-level badge absence. Gates: `vitest run` **1221/1221** (111 files; +18 acceptance in `skills-projection.test.tsx`) · typecheck · eslint · prettier on every changed file (the `skills-staging/` warnings are the standing pre-existing untracked-tree condition recorded at M0) · coverage 92.43/84.02/89.31/92.43 over the 80/75/78/80 floors · Vite production build green. **Pin-strength evidence:** an 8-mutation matrix ran with per-mutation predictions written BEFORE each run, and every mutation reddened exactly its predicted pins — neutralization gate dropped → 6 red (M24-FS-1/2/3/5a/5b + CNT-1); suppression count forced 0 → 5 red; join-map readiness gate dropped → 1 red (BDG-1, whose not-ready arm deliberately passes data with a non-ready status so data presence alone can never satisfy it); emit order moved → 3 red (URL-1, FS-5a/5b); reset fields dropped → 1 red (RST-1); provenance condition dropped → 1 red (PRV-1); scope filter line dropped → 3 red; a skills id leaked into searchText → 1 red (SEQ-1) — tree restored and re-verified green after each. Orientation note (2026-09-02): the 31 post-M2.3 commits on origin/main touched only the four data artifacts (`stars.json`, `dataset-meta.json`, `ai-annotations{,-meta}.json`) — no drift into any M2.4 surface; classification artifacts remain the 2026-08 generation, which is exactly the §2.1 steady-state the soft note exists for.

**Pre-commit review round 1 (2026-09-02; luna@max + luna@ultra + sol@max on a 200KB full-content packet; all identities invocation-confirmed) = 3/3 FIX; every finding author-reproduced or adjudicated with recorded counter-evidence; fixed in-slice; focused round 2 dispatched on the amended diff.** **F-A (3/3 convergent — the round's real find):** readiness was FAIL-OPEN at two NEW surfaces — RepositoryView's status-less data fallback (`skillsStatus ? … : skillsClassification != null`) and `dashboardToView`'s `skillsReady = true` default — so classification DATA alone could activate projection, violating the charter's "status `ready` is the sole activation condition".
